不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
黃金類股：央行配置需求仍強，惟 ETF 資金流出壓抑短線表現
7 月金價保持在區間內震盪整理，儘管美元走強及美債殖利率攀升持續為金價帶來壓力，然而來
自全球央行的買盤也如我們先前所料的為金價帶來了有力的支撐。
世界黃金協會報告指出，2026 年第二季，全球黃金總需求為 1,269 噸，與 2025 年同期持平；
累計 2026 年上半年，全球黃金總需求年增 2%至 2,522 噸，需求金額創下 3,800 億美元新高。與此
同時 ，2026 年第二季倫敦金銀市場協會黃金午後定盤價平均為每盎司 4,506.29 美元 ， 雖較第一季下

跌 8%，但仍較 2025 年同期上漲 37%。這顯示第二季金價雖自高檔回落，但全球黃金需求並未同步
轉弱，尤其央行買盤回升更成為支撐市場的主要力量。
第二季全球央行合計購買 289 噸黃金，歷經第一季的放緩後，購金規模再次重新回到過去四年
的高檔水準。高盛分析則預估，全球央行 5 月購金量達到 81 噸，其中中國購買 48 噸，是當月最大
的可識別買家；經季節調整後，全球央行近三個月月均購金量達到 67 噸，約為 2022 年前每月 17
噸平均值的四倍 。 上述數據顯示 ， 即使在金價走勢低迷的狀況下，全球央行對於黃金的承接力道仍強 。
進一步觀察央行持續購金的原因，官方貨幣與金融機構論壇（OMFIF）2026 年針對 74 家央行
及 16 家公共退休與主權基金進行的調查顯示，儲備多元化仍是央行持有黃金最主要的原因。此外，
有 51%的受訪者將防範地緣政治風險列為考量，比例較 2024 年增加 11 個百分點；同時，79%的受
訪央行認為全球貨幣體系正朝向多極化發展 。調查結果顯示， 各國央行正逐步降低對單一儲備貨幣的

依賴，並增持黃金以因應制裁、資產凍結及貨幣體系分裂風險，由此也可見，本波全球央行買盤更接
近長期儲備調整，而非短線對於金價的追逐。
然而對於利率較為敏感的私人投資資金 ， 降低黃金配置趨勢仍持續為金價帶來明顯壓力。 世界黃
金協會數據顯示，2026 年第二季全球黃金 ETF 淨流出 45 噸，6 月單月再流出 89 億美元；其中，北
美黃金 ETF 上半年累計流出 77 億美元。造成北美資金流出的主因，在於聯準會態度偏向鷹派，加上
美伊衝突推升通膨疑慮，帶動實質利率與美元走高，進而提高持有黃金的機會成本。
展望後市，儘管短線上金價表現持續承壓，然而中長線看，當前市場已定價到明年底前 Fed 將
升息 2 碼的鷹派走勢，若後續美伊衝突有所緩和，使市場升息預期降溫，黃金 ETF 資金賣壓有望出
現改善， 屆時搭配央行長期買盤 ，黃金仍有望重啟新一波漲勢，故我們對黃金類股後市維持中性看法 。

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：Fed 鷹派暫停推升長率，投資仍以 3~10 年期美債/投級債為主
FOMC 7/29 以 9 比 3 票將聯邦基金利率目標區間維持於 3.50%～3.75%。Hammack、Kashkari
及 Logan 三名委員反對維持利率不變，主張本次即升息 1 碼。會後聲明指出美國經濟活動仍以穩健
速度擴張，資本投資及生產力成長強勁，但通膨仍高於 2%目標，部分反映能源等供給衝擊。
Fed 主席 Warsh 強調，本次按兵不動並非聯準會行動遲緩或政策慣性，並重申不存在高於 2%
的 「軟性通膨目標」 。超過五年的高通膨不可能因單月物價小幅下降便獲得解決，聯準會將持續履行

物價穩定責任，必要時不會猶豫採取行動。華許同時指出，距離上次會議的 42 天內，美債殖利率曲
線各期限的名目與實質殖利率均明顯上升，部分升幅已位居近 20 年的前十分位數。他認為，聯準會
減少前瞻指引後，市場開始依據經濟數據自行調整價格。此番談話並非表示聯準會受制於市場，而是
暗示金融市場利率上升已產生部分緊縮效果，使本次不必立即升息。華許指出，AI 相關高科技設備
及軟體投資近四季增長率接近 20%，有助於製造業產出及未來生產力，但記憶體、邏輯晶片及相關
AI 基礎建設價格亦同步上升。FOMC 已開始討論，相關漲價究竟只是特定產業的供給衝擊，或可能
演變為更廣泛的通膨壓力。
債市對此次 FOMC 決議的反應呈現長端領跌、殖利率曲線明顯陡峭化。此一走勢顯示，市場雖
因 Fed 未立即升息而降低短端利率壓力，卻同步提高對能源通膨、財政供給、期限溢酬及政策可信
度的風險定價。利率期貨對 9 月升息的定價在決議後一度升至約 75%，稍後回落至約 6 成，代表市
場並未排除升息，而是等待後續通膨與就業數據確認。8 月沒有例行 FOMC 利率會議，債市定價將

更加依賴經濟數據。由於 Warsh 減少前瞻指引，單一數據對升息機率及短端殖利率的影響可能較過
去放大，預期 8 月殖利率仍將維持高檔震盪。
8 月債市的核心矛盾在於「殖利率已具吸引力，但長端風險尚未完全穩定」。我們認為偏高的殖
利率可提供較佳息收及價格緩衝；若油價維持高檔、核心通膨降溫停滯或美國公債供給增加， 20～
30 年期債券仍可能承受期限溢酬上升壓力。貝萊德亦指出，目前美債實質殖利率已具吸引力，但收
益機會仍應透過存續期間控制與券種選擇取得，而非全面延長年期。
在未來聯準會減少前瞻指引 、 利率路徑更依賴數據環境下 ， 市場對聯準會政策預期的調整頻率將
提高，預期債券市場波動性亦將隨之升溫。投資人對 Fed 升息疑慮或導致短天期債券殖利率出現較
大波動，且存續期間過短亦面臨再投資風險；長天期債券又有期限溢酬偏高、政府財政惡化及公債供
給增加等風險。因此，建議債券布局可聚焦 3 至 10 年期美國公債及投等債，不僅能提供相對具吸
引力的息收，亦能降低短端政策波動及長端期限溢酬風險。

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 7 日(水)

1/3 
9 月会合以降に日銀を取り巻く状況が変わった印象  
 経済調査部主席エコノミスト藤代宏一 
（℡：050-5474-6123）

・時事通信は10月６日付で「日銀が『基調的な物価上昇率』について、２％程度に到達したと
判断する方向で検討に入ることが５日、分かった」と報じた。記事内には10月29・30日開催
の金融政策決定会合で取りまとめる展望レポートで、そうした認識が記述されるとしてい
る。

・筆者はこの報道が現実になる可能性が高いと判断している。日銀は予てから基調的物価上昇
率が２％に到達する時期を「2026年度後半から2027年度」と予想してきたので（2026年度後
半にあたる）10月にそうした認識を示さないと、ハト派的な印象を与えてしまうことにな
る。

・既に日銀の政策態度は基調的物価上昇率を２％に「引き上げる」段階から、２％以上に「上
振れさせない」段階に移行している。局面が変化した理由としては、やはり原油高とそれに
対する企業の価格設定行動の積極化が大きいだろう。従来、原油高は企業収益を圧迫するこ
とで、賃上げを阻害し、個人消費の停滞に繋がってきた。そうした環境下で内需は拡大しな
いため、原油高は短期的にインフレ要因となっても、やや長い目でみるとデフレ的な色彩を
帯びていた。もっとも、2026年春先以降の原油高局面では、政府の手厚い支援もあり、企業
・日経平均株価は先行き12 ヶ月72,000 円程度で推移するだろう 
・USD/JPY は先行き12 ヶ月 155 円程度で推移するだろう 
・日銀は政策金利を12 月に 1.5％とした後、27 年末までに2.25％とするだろう 
・FED はFF 金利を、年内は4.0％で据え置くだろう

### Page 2

2/3 
は価格転嫁を積極的に進めた。企業物価指数は４月に前月比＋2.8％と即座に上昇し、前年比
伸び率は８月時点でも＋7.6％と高止まりしている。日銀短観（６月および９月調査）では１
年後の販売価格見通しが急伸した。こうした早期かつ大幅な価格転嫁の進展によって、企業
収益は傷みにくくなっている。このことは、名目賃金と名目消費が伸びやすい構図にあるこ
とを浮き彫りにしている。

・日銀が、基調的物価上昇率が２％に到達したとの認識を示すのであれば、それは利上げペー
スの加速を自ら正当化することになる。さすがに10月の利上げはビハインド・ザ・カーブに
陥ったことを認めたと市場参加者に受け止められる恐れがあるため、見送られると予想する
が、（前回利上げから約３ヶ月が経過する）12月会合では利上げが決定されるだろう。そう
なれば、３～４ヶ月に一度の利上げが標準ペースとなることが市場参加者に広く意識される
だろう。

・利上げを巡っては、経済指標に加えて「政府との距離感」を考慮する必要があり、予想は難
儀であった。しかしながら、ここ数週間で情勢は変化したように感じる。９月25日に片山さ
つき財務相が「官邸とも相談の上、私から初めて申し上げるが、先日の日米首脳会談では、
為替について、トランプ大統領から円安への懸念が示された」と発言したことを皮切りに、
同日には城内経済再生相が「アベノミクス的な意味でのリフレーション政策は、もうそうい
う局面は終わっている」とした。また10月５日に公表された９月30日の経済財政諮問会議の
議事要旨では、民間議員である若田部昌澄氏が「デフレではない状況になりつつある現状で
リフレ政策が終焉を迎えているというのは当然のことだ」との見解を示し、さらに９月の金
融政策決定会合で反対票を投じた佐藤綾野日銀政策委員は６日までに共同通信の単独インタ

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
ビューに応じて「（政策金利を）段階的に調整していく方針には賛成している」とした。９
月18日会合における「主な意見」で内閣府の出席者（おそらく城内大臣）が利上げに消極的
と読める発言をしていたことと整合しないが、それは9月18日から25日の間に政府の態度が変
化した可能性が指摘できる。

・10月会合までに円安が進む、あるいは原油高等の要因によってＦｅｄが利上げに踏み切る事
態となれば、日銀も追随する可能性があるものの、そうした状況に至らなければ、10月は
「既往の利上げ効果を見極めたい」などとして利上げを見送るだろう。

・なお、これらに鑑みて、筆者は2027年７月の審議委員人事が中立ないしはタカ派的な人選に
なると予想する。高田委員と田村委員に考え方の近い人物に白羽の矢が立ち、リフレ派が送
り込まれることはないと考えている。利上げに積極的な２名の後任人事にリフレ派と目され
る人物が浮上した場合、金融市場では長期金利上昇や円安の反応が予想される。

9 月金融数据点评

关注外部环境对适度宽松货币政策的影响   
9 月新增社融和新增信贷均好于万得一致预期；企业融资需求分层，信用度
较高的企业以企业债为代表的融资需求下降，依赖贷款渠道的企业贷款 9 月
表现相对更好；居民部门加杠杆意愿不强；受资本市场波动影响，存款搬家
有变化的趋势；关注海外加息周期和高利率环境对国内金融条件和货币政策
的潜在影响。 
 9 月新增社融 3.55 万亿元，较去年同期多增 192 亿元，较 8 月多增 1.89 万亿元，
高于万得一致预期的 3.13 万亿元。9 月社融存量同比增长 7.1%，较 8 月下降了
0.07 个百分点，基本持平于万得一致预期的 7.06%。9 月社融口径新增人民币贷
款 1.27 万亿元，较去年同期少增 3342 亿元，较 8 月多增 1.22 万亿元。 
 9 月新增社融较去年同期小幅多增，主要原因是政府债券较去年同期多增了 3438
亿元，同时委托贷款和外币贷款也较去年同期有所多增；从当月新增规模看，新
增融资规模较大的项目，主要是政府债券、人民币贷款和汇票，当月少增的是信
托贷款和企业债。 值得关注的是9 月股票融资表现依然较好， 当月新增658 亿元，
较去年同期也有小幅上升， 但企业债少增425 亿元， 表明企业融资需求依然不强。 
 从社融存量结构来看，2026 年 9 月与 8 月相比，占比上升的有政府债券（上升
0.17 个百分点）和汇票（上升 0.04 个百分点），占比下降的有股票融资（下降
0.01 个百分点）、信托贷款（下降 0.02 个百分点）、企业债（下降 0.08 个百分
点）和人民币贷款（下降 0.16 个百分点）。与去年同期相比，9 月占比上升较多
的是政府债券（上升 1.30 个百分点）和企业债（上升 0.16 个百分点），占比下
降较多的是人民币贷款 （下降1.31 个百分点） 和委托贷款 （下降0.13 个百分点） 。
虽然新增企业债融资规模少增，但企业债融资占比仍有上升趋势。 
 9 月 M2 同比增长 7.2%，较 8 月下降 0.3 个百分点；M1 同比增长 3.2%，较 8 月
下降 0.9 个百分点；M0 同比增长 10.6%，较 8 月下降 0.6 个百分点。9 月活期存
款（M1-M0）环比上升 0.79%，定期存款（M2-M1）环比上升 0.76%，与去年同
期相比，活期存款环比增速偏低，定期存款环比增速偏高，关注资本市场指数波
动对存款变化趋势的影响。 
 9 月关注非银存款回流。9 月金融机构新增存款 1.77 万亿元，多增的主要是居民
存款 2.40 万亿元和企业存款 1.31 万亿元，财政存款和非银存款均少增；与去年
同期相比，9 月新增存款少增 4400 亿元，其中非银存款少增 8500 亿元，居民存
款少增 5600 亿元。9 月新增存款不弱， 但与去年同期相较不强， 其中值得关注的
细节较多：一是企业存款多增，一定程度上印证了企业融资需求不强的原因，二
是居民存款较去年同期少增，关注居民收入增速变化情况，三是财政存款较去年
同期多增， 关注财政支出是否偏慢， 以及对固投增速的影响， 四是非银存款偏弱，
可能代表投资者对资本市场的态度短期偏谨慎。 
 9 月实体经济融资需求延续偏弱。9 月金融机构口径新增人民币贷款 1.08 万亿元，
略高于万得一致预期的 9880 亿元。 其中， 新增短贷及票据3200 亿元， 中长贷9774
亿元， 企业贷1.29 万亿元。 我们认为有三处值得关注， 一是虽然新增贷款超预期，
但是较去年同期少增，主要是居民贷款少增较多，二是新增企业贷款较多，与企
业债发行融资需求偏弱有所不同，三是居民短贷和企业短贷均较去年同期少增较
多。我们认为贷款数据指向了居民部门加杠杆需求不强，以及企业部门内部出现
分化，其中信用等级较高的企业融资需求不强，但依靠信贷的企业中长期贷款需
求仍在。 
 关注外部环境对适度宽松货币政策的影响。9 月底以来，央行货币政策出台了三
项主要调整，一是央行等八部门联合印发《关于金融支持服务业扩能提质的指导
意见》，二是 9 月 29 日发布调整完善若干货币政策工具，包括下调抵押补充贷
款（PSL）利率 0.25 个百分点，将“六张网”建设纳入抵押补充贷款支持领域，及
再贷款扩容等，三是强化财政金融政策协同，实施居民购房贷款贴息政策。整体
来看适度宽松的货币政策仍在发力，但货币政策继续宽松的约束也在加强：从国
内方面看，9 月企业新发放贷款加权平均利率略低于 3%，比上年同期低约 0.2 个
百分点，个人住房新发放贷款加权平均利率约为 3.1%，与上年同期基本持平，9
月底银行间隔夜拆借利率在 1.36%的较低水平，十年期国债利率在 1.68%的较低
水平，人民币对美元汇率增长 10 月初升值突破 6.7；从海外看，市场方面，9 月
底十年美债收益率达到 5.29%的较高水平，同时美联储已于 9 月落地一次加息，
并且纪要显示年内仍将有一次加息。我们认为下一阶段需要关注“适度宽松”的
货币政策可能受外部环境影响，出现国内金融条件“被动收缩”的情况。 
 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行；欧美经济回落速度偏快；国际局势复杂化。

本报告准确表述了证券分析师的个人观点。该证券分析师声明，本人未在公司内、外部机构兼任有损本人独立性与客
观性的其他职务，没有担任本报告评论的上市公司的董事、监事或高级管理人员；也不拥有与该上市公司有关的任何
财务权益；本报告评论的上市公司或其它第三方都没有或没有承诺向本人提供与本报告有关的任何补偿或其它利益。  
中银国际证券股份有限公司同时声明，将通过公司网站披露本公司授权公众媒体及其他机构刊载或者转发证券研究报
告有关情况 。 如有投资者于未经授权的公众媒体看到或从其他机构获得本研究报告的 ， 请慎重使用所获得的研究报告，
以防止被误导，中银国际证券股份有限公司不对其报告理解和使用承担任何责任。  
评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

### Page 1

(373220) 
LG 에너지솔루션 
2026.10.12

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

보상금 제외해도 호실적 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 530,000원(상향) 
 현재주가 401,000원(10/8)

 [투자의견] 당사는 LG에너지솔루션에 대해 실적 추정치 상향을 반영하여 목
표주가를 530,000원으로 13% 상향하고, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 
3Q26부터 ESS 물량 증가에 따른 가동률 상승 효과가 본격적으로 실적에 
반영되기 시작했으며, 4Q26에도 AMPC 제외 흑자 기조를 이어갈 전망이
다. 전사 가동률은 3분기 65%까지 상승한 것으로 파악되며, 이에 따른 펀
더멘털 개선이 본격화되고 있다고 판단한다. 
 [3Q26 잠정 실적 Review] 매출액 9.6조원(+28%qoq, +59%yoy), 영업이
익 7,560억원(+567%qoq, +26%yoy)을 기록하며 당사 추정을 큰 폭으로 
상회했다. 북미 생산보조금(AMPC)을 제외한 매출액은 9.2조원, 영업이익은 
3,391억원(영업이익률 3.7%)으로, 2 분기(-1,277억원, -1.7%) 대비 흑자 
전환에 성공했다. ① 원통형은 테슬라향 물량 호조에 힘입어 영업이익 
2,010억원(영업이익률 8.1%)을 달성한 것으로 추정된다. ② EV는 유럽 폴
란드 공장의 가동률 상승과 GM 얼티엄의 8월 가동 개시로 매출이 증가했
다. 여기에 완성차 업체 보상금 3,000억원이 반영되며 영업이익 1,090억
원(영업이익률 2.5%)을 기록한 것으로 추정된다. ③ ESS는 매출액이 50% 
이상 급증하며 실적 서프라이즈를 견인했다. AMPC 포함 영업이익은 4,460
억원(영업이익률 16.0%)으로 추정된다. 2 분기 매출 성장률 +17%qoq에 
이어 3분기 성장세가 더욱 가팔라졌다는 점이 긍정적이다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 8.0조원(-17%qoq, +30%yoy), 영업이익 6,790
억원(-10%qoq, 흑전yoy)으로 추정한다. 4 분기 실적은 ESS 수익성 개선 
속도와 완성차 업체 보상금 반영 규모에 따라 변동할 전망이다. 동사는 기존 
가이던스를 통해 하반기 북미 ESS 생산량이 상반기 대비 2배 증가할 것으
로 제시한 바 있다. 3분기 ESS는 AMPC를 제외하더라도 양호한 수익성을 
기록한 것으로 추정된다. 향후 팩 생산 병목 해소와 북미 공장 가동 안정화
에 따른 고정비 부담 완화가 이어지며 추가적인 손익 개선이 가능할 전망이
다. EV 역시 GM 얼티엄 생산 확대와 유럽 공장 가동률 상승이 지속되며 
AMPC 제외 적자 규모를 점진적으로 축소할 것으로 예상한다. 
 [투자포인트] 9월 미시간 랜싱 공장의 개소식이 개최됐다. 랜싱 공장은 당초 
EV용 파우치 배터리 생산거점으로 계획됐으나 ESS 중심으로 역할이 재편
됐으며, 내년에는 10GWh 이상의 각형 LFP 생산라인이 추가될 예정이다. 
2027년에는 신규 대규모 증설보다 기존 북미 생산거점의 가동률 상승과 
ESS 생산량 확대를 통한 수익성 개선이 핵심이 될 전망이다. 북미 전력망 
접속 지연과 AI 데이터센터의 부하 변동성 확대에 따라 Stand-alone ESS, 
BTM, UPS, BBU 수요가 증가하면서 신규 수주도 지속 확대될 것으로 예상
한다. 특히 동사의 Non-PFE 요건 충족과 SI· O&M을 아우르는 End-to-
End 솔루션 역량이 북미 ESS 시장에서 경쟁력으로 작용할 전망이다.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%) 
ㆍSTRONG BUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +50%이상 3% 
ㆍBUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +15%이상 ~ +50%미만 94% 
ㆍHOLD(중립) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%이상 ∼ +15%미만 3% 
ㆍREDUCE(매도) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%미만 0% 
 (2026.09.30 기준)

Slide continues
1 October 2026
Key points
• The Cotality home value index –
covering all of Australia – fell 1.1% in
September following a downwardly
revised declines of 1.2% in August

and 1.4% in July. Annual growth
stalled flat with prices nationally now
5.2% below their peak in March.
• Declines continue to be widespread.
Across the major cities, prices fell
1.4% in Sydney, 1.5% in Brisbane,
1.3% in Adelaide, and 1.2% in Perth
with Melbourne recording a slightly
milder 0.7% decline. Sydney and
Melbourne continue to see more
pronounced declines with prices now
down 7%yr and 6.2%yr respectively.
• Preliminary estimates show the number

of sales, in rolling 3mth terms, down
3.9%qtr and 17.4%yr. The pace of
turnover falls has moderated from
the rapid 8.6%qtr recorded in July.

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The Cotality home value index, covering all of Australia,
fell 1.1% in September with downward revisions to the
previous two months again shaving a further 0.4% off prices.
The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2% from their

March peak. Annual growth is flat.
The price correction remains broad, Cotality reporting 97% of
capital city suburbs experienced falls over the three months
to September, Darwin the only city to record gains. Sydney
and Melbourne continue to see more pronounced declines,
albeit moderating a touch, particularly in Melbourne.
Against this, declines in Brisbane, Adelaide and Perth have
accelerated over the last few months. Across segments, unit
prices and lower price tiers continue to track slightly milder

falls than houses and top tiers.
Seasonal variations accentuated price weakness through winter
but are now neutral. The peak-to-trough price decline nationally
is slightly milder at 4.6% in seasonally adjusted terms. Unless
specified, figures in titles, tables and the commentary below are
in non-seasonally adjusted terms, consistent with the ‘headline’
figures reported by Cotality. However, figures shown in charts
are in seasonally adjusted terms.

Preliminary estimates of turnover show rolling 3mth volumes
down 3.9%qtr in September and 17.4%yr with previous
estimates revised 1-2ppts lower. The monthly pattern
suggests small gains in July and August have given way to
another sizeable fall in September.
Looking at the price performance across different jurisdictions:
• Sydney prices dropped 1.4% fall in September, monthly
declines averaging 1.6% over the previous four months,
tracking a 17% annualised pace of decline since May. All

segments and tiers recorded falls in Q3 but with top and
middle tier houses notably weaker.
• Melbourne prices fell 0.7%mth, a slower pace than other
capitals. This likely reflects the weaker starting point coming
into the correction, prices already back to the levels seen in
mid-2021. As elsewhere, 'top tier' houses prices are leading
the decline. 'Low tier' unit prices mostly held their ground in
Q3, prices in this sub-segment already plumbing 10yr lows.

• Brisbane was the weakest capital city price-wise, recording
a 1.5% drop in September with previous monthly declines
also revised down heavily (August from 1% to 1.6% and
July from 1.2% to 1.7%). The combined effect has taken
the rolling 3mth change to –4.7%qtr from –2.7%qtr last
month and –0.6%qtr in July. At 5.9%yr, annual growth is
still positive but losing altitude quickly, having run at 18%yr
as recently as May. Sellers have been slow to respond to

3WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
the sudden stop with total listings up over 50% vs a year
ago, albeit still not high compared to long run averages
(reflecting the extremely tight starting point for the Brisbane
market).
• Adelaide dwelling prices fell 1.3%mth, the August decline

also marked down from 0.8% to 1.1%, annual price growth
dropping to 6.5%yr. The price correction began later in
Adelaide but is now running at a similar pace to prices
nationally.
• Perth recorded a 1.2% decline but also saw big downward
revisions to previous months (August from 0.8% to –1.7%
and July from –1.3% to –1.8%). At 10.1%yr, annual growth
is still solidly positive but was tracking at over 25%yr earlier
in the year. As with Adelaide and Brisbane, the detail shows

a more even spread of declines across price tiers. However,
turnover is showing a milder pull-back in Adelaide.
• The smaller capitals were again more mixed, price growth
still positive in Darwin (0.4%mth, 11.9%yr), relatively
resilient in Hobart (–0.5%mth, 7%yr) but weaker in
Canberra (–1.1%mth, –1.6%yr).
• Regional markets showed a 0.9% decline in Qld but slightly
milder declines in NSW (–0.7%) and Victoria (0.3%). Annual
prices growth is in the 3.4-6.3%yr range.

Our recently released Housing Pulse report (here) provides
a deeper diver into the situation in Australia's housing
markets. While the absence of selling pressure, still tight
supply-demand balance and sentiment mix suggests the
correction was still relatively 'orderly' through the September
quarter, the latest monthly data shows momentum remains
unambiguously negative.
That said, there are clearly more headwinds to come. The
RBA latest interest rate rise and what now looks to be a

more likely prospect of a follow-up increase at its meeting in
early November will impact in coming months. Next week's
consumer sentiment update will show how the change, and
the latest round of weak updates, is shaping housing-related
sentiment. Either way, momentum looks set to remain weak
into year-end and, barring a significantly improved inflation
and interest rate outlook, early 2027.

Document 184: Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August (2026-10-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August (2026-10-17).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 13
Status: ok

### Page 1

#trade #australia
Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August
By Ryan Wells
Economist, Westpac
13:19 October 01 2026 Share
Another surge in data centre-related equipment imports in August, alongside continued
volatility in gold trade. Trade balance: $0.5bn surplus. Export values: 3.7%mth. Import
values: 5.8%mth.

### Page 2

Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August from $1.4bn in July (revised down from
$1.9bn previously). This result was significantly lower than expected – the market consensus was for a
broadly stable reading, while Westpac had a slight widening pencilled in. Over the past six months or
so, the trade balance has been oscillating between small deficits and surpluses, largely coinciding with
significant month-to-month volatility in the trade mix. The chief culprit behind this volatility has been

trade in non-monetary gold, among other components.
Before discussing the detail, it is once again worth noting the results for gold up front. In August, non-
monetary gold exports jumped 20%, while imports surged 91%. Excluding gold, the trade figures have
been much more stable, closer to a deficit of $2-3bn per month over the past three months, down from
larger deficits around the start of the year.
Export earnings rose 3.7% in August, bouncing back from a 3.6% decline in July. The trend for exports

so far this year looks a bit more positive, but again, this is tricky to read given the volatility present in
the data. Excluding gold, export earnings were up 1.6% in August. This was largely supported by a
4.7% lift in coal and a 2.8% increase in LNG, with further detail provided by the ABS suggests that an
increase in both volumes and prices contributed. However, iron ore (–1.5%) and rural goods (–1.0%)
weighed on earnings in the month.

### Page 3

The import bill meanwhile increase by 5.8% in August. Once again, a surge in automatic data
processing (ADP) equipment imports – which capture the investment upswing related to data centres –
were in the spotlight, surging 79% in the month up to $4.3bn. There were also an increase in other
capital goods imports centred on telecommunications equipment (+12%) and civil aircraft (+30%) –
large in their own right but dwarfed by the surge in ADP imports in the month.

Our latest estimates have the total pipeline for data centres at around $175bn over the coming decade,
with much of that being front-loaded. It is natural to expect month-to-month volatility in ADP equipment
imports as these large and lumpy data centre projects are worked through, but it is worth highlighting
that August’s jump closely resembles the surge from March, when imports also surged to around
$4.3bn. Given the scale of the increase in August, Q3 is shaping up to be another quarter where data

centre investment will play a significant role in driving growth – while it is true that the data centre build-
out is import-intensive, our modelling suggests that once spending enters Australia, the local economic
impact is likely to be around half of the total investment.

### Page 4

Elsewhere across the import basket, while gold imports also surged, it was effectively offset by a
decline in consumer imports of non-industrial transport equipment (i.e. cars), down 21.2% in August
following the surge from May to July. While still tentative at this stage, it suggests that the momentum in
EV purchases, which played a significant role in the consumer spending story in Q2, may be starting to
wane. Additionally, a further pull-back in fuel imports (–5.0%) in August has extended the downward

trend in recent months, following the initial surge in oil prices in March and April after the war in the
Middle East began. However, with the conflict re-escalating recently and margins on refined fuel
products widening, we could see this trend reverse over the coming months.

Document 187: Belgium’s public finances_ a missed opportunity

### Page 1

Belgium’s current failure to agree on a three-year €10bn
savings package is not yet a political crisis, but it is a missed
opportunity. A deal on the 2027 budget remains possible, yet
delaying the harder choices risks weakening the country’s
ﬁscal credibility as the 2029 elections draw closer
Philippe Ledent
Belgian PM Bart De Wever
This was supposed to be a decisive week for Belgium’s public
ﬁnances. The prime minister was due to deliver his general policy

statement to parliament on Tuesday, outlining a budget
agreement among the governing parties for the next three years,
up to the 2029 elections. In addition to measures already adopted,
the government is seeking €10bn in recurring savings, phased in
over the next three years.
A new timetable for negotiations
For now, however, the budget talks have failed to produce an
agreement. A central element of the package was a one-

### Page 2

percentage-point increase in the standard VAT rate, from 21% to
22%, but one coalition party ﬁrmly opposed the proposal. The
general policy statement therefore had to be postponed. Under
Prime Minister Bart De Wever's revised timetable, the immediate
priority is to secure agreement on the 2027 budget, including €4bn
in savings.
Ideally, a deal would be reached by the end of October, allowing
parliament to debate and approve the legislation before year-end.

This would avoid recourse to the system of provisional twelfths,
under which the previous year’s budget is rolled over on a monthly
basis to keep public services running. The PM has made clear that
he wants to avoid such an outcome at all costs. He is even prepared
to push through legislation that would trigger automatic spending
cuts of €3-4bn from January 2027 if no budget is agreed by year-
end.
In a second phase, negotiations are expected to resume with the

aim of ﬁnding a further €6bn in savings across the 2028 and 2029
budgets. This stage will be crucial for Belgium’s medium-term ﬁscal
trajectory. Postponing those discussions until next year is a risk. By
then, the federal and regional elections scheduled for spring 2029
would be closer, and the wide political diﬀerences within the current
coalition would make agreement on additional measures more
diﬃcult.
A chance to stand apart from France
There is no political crisis at this stage. With a package of measures,

Belgium should still be able to adopt a 2027 budget that brings the
deﬁcit below 5% of GDP. Even so, failure to reach a swift and
comprehensive agreement on the full €10bn package is a missed
opportunity for the federal government to show ﬁnancial markets
that Belgium is not France.
On headline indicators – public deﬁcits, debt and even economic
growth – little separates the two countries. France’s diﬃculty,
however, lies in its inability to set and pass a budget, compounded

by the political uncertainty surrounding the April 2027 presidential
election. Belgium, by contrast, has a governing majority capable of

Document 258: How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

1

SPECIAL EDITION | HOW BAS IS THE RISE 
IN BOND YIELDS?

TRANSCRIPTION

0:00:10 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Everyone, welcome. As we head into autumn 2026, interest rates are reaching levels never seen for many, many years. 
Why is this happening? Is it sustainable? And what are the implications for the economies of different countries? All these 
questions will be discussed over the next few minutes with the economists of BNP Paribas. Let us begin with Isabelle 
Mateos y Lago, chief economist of the group. Hello, Isabelle.  
0:00:38 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everyone. 
0:00:39 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you for being with us today. Just to begin, a simple explanation. What rates are we talking about and which countries 
are affected? 
0:00:48 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, it is very important to clarify this. So, we are talking about long-term interest rates, rates at 10 years or longer 
maturities, 30 years, etc. The rates at which sovereign states borrow on the market. And this is primarily something that is 
happening in advanced countries, so United States, United King dom, Japan, and then the Eurozone countries. And indeed, 
as you said, we are seeing since the beginning of the summer, interest rates reach levels we had not seen for about 20 
years. But it is important to keep in mind that up until that point, up until the mid -2000s, the level of interest rates that 
we're seeing now were really quite common, quite normal, so one shouldn't overdramatize.  
0:01:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why do we find ourselves in this situation and how did we get to this point?  
0:01:41 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, so the direct cause is that investors are demanding higher interest rates in order to  lend their capital to governments. 
But why is this? Well, there's essentially three reasons for this, and not all of them are bad, and they're not mutually 
exclusive either. The top reason to my mind is that there's just greater competition for capital be tween sovereign states 
and the corporate sector, who are all facing simultaneously additional investment needs, in defense, in artificial 
intelligence, in energy transition, in resilience. And so,  you just have greater competition for capital and that pushes up the 
price of capital, which is the interest rate. 
Reason number two is because markets have revised upwards their expectations of how high central banks are going to be 
taking their interest rates. 
And this mechanically translates into higher rates at the long end. And then there's a third reason, which doesn't apply 
necessarily in every case, but which is essentially higher sovereign risk, a kind of a risk premium, in cases where there are  
concerns arising about the sustainability of these public debts.

### Page 2

2

0:02:59 - Emmanuel Laborde 
What are the implications for global economies? 
0:03:03 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
So that's exactly what we'll be discussing in the next segments. But basically, the two key issues are, one, an adverse 
impact on public finances. It's higher interest rates making it harder to balance the books in terms of public accounts. And 
then secondly, these higher rates tend to slow down economic growth. Of course, by how much and how serious it will be, 
depends on how high interest rates go and how long they stay there. But that's for the next segments.  
0:03:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Yes. Thank you very much, Isabelle. Having said the scene now, let's move on to the first segment with the following 
question. What makes debt sustainable? And let's see what Hélène Baudchon has to say.  
Hello Hélène and welcome into this special edition. 
0:03:58 - Hélène Baudchon 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everybody. 
0:04:01 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When economists talk about public debt, they often refer to the sustainability of debt. As an economist, can you explain to 
us what that means and what is the debt trajectory deemed unsustainable?  
0:04:15 - Hélène Baudchon 
To begin with, I will say that a temporary increase in debt can be seen as a normal evolution, not particularly concerning, 
for instance during an economic crisis or more recently during a health crisis. In such cases, it acts as a buffer. A problem  
arises when the debt level never comes back down. An unsustainable trajectory can be therefore defined, characterized by 
a continuous regular rise in the debt level, both in absolute terms and as a proportion of GDP, driven by high and recurring 
fiscal deficits. And sometimes interest payments can exacerbate the problem.  
0:05:00 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In very simple terms, you are talking about the snowball effect.  
0:05:03 - Hélène Baudchon 
Exactly explaining why consolidation measures are needed in order to prevent such an adverse situation from occurring 
and in order to prevent, in fact, a sudden loss of confidence from creditors.  
0:05:16 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Regarding governments, what should serve as a warning sign for them? And at what point could the situation become, let's 
say, problematic? 
0:05:27 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, simply speaking, when interest rates rise, not so long ago, up until 2022, Eurozone countries were borrowing at very 
low rates and in some instance, even negative rates. And although debt levels were already at high level at that time, the 
low rates helped manage debt servicing. Not much fiscal effort was needed to keep the public debt ratios in check.  
0:05:55 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Of course, we all remember this because it was a very unusual situation, but now things are very different.

### Page 3

3

0:06:01 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed, the situation today is clearly more challenging simply because bond yields are significantly higher and they 
are rising at the time of our discussion. This is for the part the result of the ECB's monetary tightening in 2022 -2023 aimed 
at addressing the inflation shock then. Then long-term interest rates have risen as a consequence and have aligned more 
closely with GDP growth rate. And in technical terms, this is what we refer to as a narrowing of the "r" - "g" spread. 
0:06:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why is this spread so important? Why do you, the economists, monitor it this way?  
0:06:48 - Hélène Baudchon 
Essentially, you have the interest rate "r", which determines the debt burden, while the growth rate "g" determines the 
trajectory of fiscal revenues. And if "r" is lower than "g", and this has been the case for many years, as you can see on the  
chart on the screen, it means that interest payments are increasing at a slower pace than the revenue component of the 
equation. 
0:07:14 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this provides some leeway in other budgetary areas, such as education, of course, defense, maybe pensions.  
0:07:22 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed. And this is what we refer to as the primary budget. However, if and when interest rates do go up while the 
economy isn't growing that much faster, then fiscal leeway diminishes.  
0:07:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Crystal clear. Now, just could you provide a few last words to wrap up the sustainability segment?  
0:07:43 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, to conclude, we are entering a period which demands greater fiscal discipline or, as some might say, less indiscipline 
in order to manage debt trajectories effectively, in particular in the countries which have high debt levels. And for instanc e, 
the Italian case is an interesting one because it is well known for its high debt level ratio. But in fact, it also has an 
advantageous position because it has no primary deficit. In fact, it even benefits from a large primary surplus.  
0:08:22 - Emmanuel Laborde 
As for the countries... For the other countries... I stop you because this will be a discussion with Guillaume Derrien during  
the next segment. Thank you very much, Hélène. 
0:08:30 - Hélène Baudchon 
Thank you, Emmanuel. 
0:08:36 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Guillaume. Welcome. 
0:08:37 - Guillaume Derrien 
Thank you. 
0:08:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Emmanuel, and hello to everyone. The flexibility of fiscal policy depends heavily on interest rates, which have 
increased considerably over the past few months. The question is, where do we stand today?

### Page 4

4

0:08:52 - Guillaume Derrien 
So, long-term rates have increased in all advanced economies, reaching levels not seen since 2008 in Europe and in the 
United States, and even since 1996 in Japan. Fortunately, the effective rate that states pay on their debt is lower than thos e 
headline figures suggest, simply because the average maturity of debt is relatively long. Precisely how long are we talking 
about? So, it's about 10 years in Japan, 13 years in the United Kingdom, and 8.5 years in France. That means that most of 
the debt was raised before rates started climbing. 
For example, in France, the effective rate just recently rose above the 2% mark, which is significantly lower than the 10 -
year bond yield, which exceeded 4% for new issuances.  
That said, in the United States, which has an average maturity of roughly six years, is experiencing a more rapid increase in  
its effective rate. 
0:09:54 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this rate increase is happening in the context of high debt -to-GDP ratios and with modest growth, correct? Yes, that's 
correct. 
0:10:05 - Guillaume Derrien 
Debt levels have reached unprecedented heights since the end of World War II. And at the same time, growth is lower, 
hovering around 2% on average in the United States, 1% in Europe and 0.5% in Japan. What does that mean for debt 
servicing costs? So, debt servicing cost is already relatively high, but it is expected to grow further as debt issue before the 
2022 rate hikes mature and are replaced with new instruments bearing higher interest rates. By 2030, interest costs could 
rise to levels last seen in 2000, specifically in France, around 4% of GDP. 
Are all the countries equally affected by these trends? Actually, no. Some countries are managing to reduce their debt 
level. 
Spain, Italy, even Japan have sufficiently improved their primary balance to achieve this, at least for the time being. And o f 
course, we must speak about Germany. Yes, Germany is implementing investment programs that result in a higher public 
deficit and debt-to-GDP ratio. But this ratio started from a relatively low base at around above 60% of GDP. That said, in 
this current context, German bonds have risen. And because German bonds are used as a benchmark for Europe, it  raises 
rates across all Eurozone countries. Is France the only country seeing its public debt go up?  
No, it's not, Emmanuel. This is happening in the United Kingdom and in the United States. These economies, like France, 
are running public deficits that are ever close or exceed 5% of GDP, which is too large for debt ratio to stabilize on its ow n. 
France and the United Kingdom are trying to gradually reduce their public deficit so as not to compromise growth. The 
United States is not going on that path yet, but market pressure could eventually force them to do so.  
0:12:08 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much for your input, Guillaume. Thank you, Emmanuel. Let's go to the next segment. It's about the impact 
of rising bond yields on financing and investments in the Eurozone.  
0:12:26 - Thomas Humblot 
Hello, Thomas. Welcome. Hello. Thank you for having me today.  
0:12:30 - Emmanuel Laborde 
We've just seen what's behind the rise in Eurozone bond yields. This rise is partly attributed to the sovereign spread. What 
impact will this have on the economy? 
0:12:42 - Thomas Humblot 
Actually, this widening of some government bond spreads is obviously not good news, but it tends paradoxically to have 
less impact than an increase in long-term interest rates that does not include the sovereign risk premium. for example, in 
France, for a very long time, it has been considered that the 10 -year OAT was the relevant benchmark for fixed loans. And 
at that time, the spread on French government bonds was relatively stable. But since 2024, that spread increased

### Page 5

5

significantly and that renders the sovereign yield a less relevant benchmark.  
And now the most relevant benchmark is the swap rate because it determines the cost of bank funding that backs new 
loans. 
0:13:56 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Since the end of 2024, we have seen a rise in long-term 10-year swap rates. What impact does this have on bank lending 
rates? 
0:14:08 - Thomas Humblot 
Fixed loans have obviously become more expensive, but to a lesser extent than if they were priced solely based on long -
term sovereign rates. And what about existing fixed-rate loans? 
There is no impact. An increase in rates for the entire maturity of a loan does not have any effect if it is a fixed -rate loan. 
Borrowers with outstanding loans are therefore protected. Obviously, new fixed loans are granted at higher rates. And this 
sharply contrasts with a floating-rates loans. 
Because with floating-rates loans, borrowers experience regular rate adjustments throughout the term of the loan. And in 
terms of bank financing? Strictly in terms of bank financing, an increase in short -term rates has an immediate and 
widespread restrictive impact on borrowers, while for long-term interest rates, the impact is more gradual, and it comes 
gradually as the outstanding amount of fixed-rate loans are progressively renewed. And the thing is that since the 
beginning of the war in Iran, we have seen a combination of the two.  
And in practical terms, the rate of new bank loans to non-financial corporations has increased about 25 basis points in the 
eurozone since the beginning of the year. 
0:16:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Just a few words about companies that borrow directly from the market.  
0:16:16 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, in that case, the impact is even more straightforward. The rate at which a company borrows from the market is the 
risk-free rate plus a premium. 
0:16:28 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Have risk premiums, known as corporate credit spreads, also increased?  
0:16:34 - Thomas Humblot 
Yes, they have increased temporarily at the beginning of the year but have returned to normal since. For the time being, the 
widening remains moderate for the highest-rated investment-grade issuers. 
However, some significant bond issuance in the euro debt market due to some major US technology companies has 
increased spreads for longer maturities. 
0:17:11 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In particular terms, how does this affect the investment decisions of the companies?  
0:17:19 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, investments that were previously worthwhile due to returns sufficient to cover the cost of capital may no longer do so 
and in turn that might lead to a decrease in demand for financing.  
0:17:40 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much Thomas. Now let us move on to the emerging economies. And it is with Cynthia Kalasopatan -Antoine.

### Page 6

6

Hello Cynthia, and welcome. 
0:17:54 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Hi, Emmanuel. Hello, everyone. 
0:17:57 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Long-term bond yields rose significantly in advanced economies over the summer. What can we now say about emerging 
markets? 
0:18:06 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Emerging bond markets held up well, which is quite unusual. The rise in long -term sovereign yields in emerging countries 
has been less pronounced than in advanced economies, including the United States and Germany. Now, there are two 
main exceptions, Turkey and Poland. Meanwhile, we did not witness any significant correction in emerging financial 
markets over the summer, and the main currencies appreciated slightly against the dollar or the euro.  
0:18:37 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Does this mean that public finances are more robust in emerging countries?  
0:18:43 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, it depends. You see, in cases like the Czech Republic and South Korea, public debt is moderate and macroeconomic 
fundamentals are solid. However, these countries are now less frequently considered as emerging and tend to be viewed 
as newly advanced economies. By contrast, Brazil and South Africa have weaker public finances and investors have 
already incorporated a higher sovereign risk into their assessments. And this is reflected in the bond yields. And this 
assessment of sovereign risk did not really change over the summer. 
0:19:26 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When comparing the main fiscal metrics, what are the trends? What do you see as an economist?  
0:19:33 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
On average, the fiscal metrics in emerging markets are not substantially worse than those in advanced economies. The 
average budget deficit levels are similar, ranging from 4% to 5% of GDP in 2025 -2026, according to the IMF. And as in 
advanced economies, the public debt is higher than its pre-Covid level. However, the average public debt to GDP ratio in 
emerging countries excluding China is close to 60%, whereas in advanced economies, it is above 100%.  
0:20:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Do you arrive at the same conclusion when comparing debt sustainability criteria?  
0:20:17 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, there are significant regional differences. In emerging Asia, the factors that determine debt sustainability are more 
often favorable than those in advanced economies. In Central Europe, the gap between the interest rate paid on debt and 
the GDP growth rate is also favorable. However, the primary deficits in some countries are high.  
0:20:45 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In a few words, a brief overview of the situation in Latin America.  
0:20:49 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, Brazil and Mexico are in a less favorable position. These countries would indeed need to generate a significant 
primary budget surplus each year in order to stabilize their debt in the medium term.

### Page 7

7

0:21:07 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Economists use the term original sin, which refers to the situation when governments resort to borrowing in foreign 
currency to address the shortfall in domestic financing. Does this phenomenon continue to distinguish emerging 
countries? 
0:21:24 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Yes, this is still the case. Emerging countries still have a significant share of their debt in foreign currency. This repres ents 
about 35 to 40% of their total debt on average. And in fact, now, this figure has decreased over the past decade, and this 
contributes to their resilience. However, governments in emerging markets have a less favorable position as regards their 
debt profile and their financing costs are also on average higher.  
0:22:02 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And finally, just a few words about a larger subject, China.  
0:22:07 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
China's public debt is significant and growing rapidly. That being said, it is denominated in domestic currency and issued in  
domestic markets. This debt is, however, sustainable in the medium term due to a very low effective interest rate and a 
high growth rate. 
0:22:29 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Cynthia, for being with us today.  
0:22:32 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Thank you. 
0:22:33 - Emmanuel Laborde 
It is now time to conclude, and we welcome back Group Chief Economist Isabelle Mateos y Lago.  
It is time for our conclusion with Isabelle. Once again, just a final question, please. Could or should, I do not know which 
word is the good one, central banks intervene to curb this rise in interest rates?  
0:22:58 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Absolutely not. The main contribution that central banks can bring to dealing with this situation is to restore price stabili ty, 
as is their mandate, as promptly as possible. And in the current circumstances, this means keeping relatively high policy 
rates, in some cases, rising them further. And the responsibility for keeping public finances on a sustainable footing rests 
squarely with governments and any attempt by these governments to instead take the easy way out by pressuring central 
banks to lower interest rates prematurely would be enormously counterproductive.  
0:23:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Isabelle. Thank you all for joining us today. Just a few words to remind you that additional economic 
research content can be found by scanning the QR code shown on your screen. Do not hesitate to use it with your 
smartphone. It is very easy. It was a pleasure to be with you today. Have a nice day and hope to see you so

SUMMARY: AI euphoria meets macro headwinds
Tech remained a major driver of equity market performance in Q3. The
Mag 7 outperformed the broader market while the S&P 500 also

posted gains, lifted by mega-cap and tech stocks.
Still, tech’s path in Q3 was volatile. July began with a reversal of the AI
trade as investors questioned elevated valuations, the sustainability of
AI spending and the outlook for semiconductor demand. That led to a
large sell-off in chip stocks, with large falls in Q2 outperformers SK
Hynix and Samsung contributing to a sharp decline in Korea’s KOSPI.
The Philly SOX experienced its worst monthly performance since

2008.
By August, however, sentiment had improved. The S&P 500 reached
fresh record highs, even as US Treasury yields rose, supported by
stronger cloud earnings and signs of AI monetization. September
proved more challenging as higher oil prices, inflation concerns and
rising bond yields weighed on risk assets, particularly semiconductors.
The Fed also raised its policy rate by 25bp on Sep 16.
Yet, despite these macro headwinds, company guidance continued to

indicate strong AI infrastructure demand, with Micron’s stronger-than-
expected revenue guidance on Sep 30 providing further evidence of
robust demand for memory chips. Memory supply remains tight,
semiconductor capex is accelerating, and the industry’s attention has
increasingly turned to bottlenecks in optical networking and photonics,
as well as concerns around AI regulation and safety. Looking ahead,
tech is likely to remain an important driver of equity returns into Q4,

although elevated valuations and tighter financial conditions leave the
sector sensitive to earnings disappointments. Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, Bloomberg Finance LP. Updated Sep 30. Returns in local currency.
Q3 started with doubts about the AI trade but ended with renewed confidence

1. GLOBAL INDUSTRY BREAKDOWN
Tech hardware, including Lenovo, Advantech, and HP Inc, rebounded in Q3. This was partly driven by accelerating AI

server demand and the ongoing data center build-out, although continuing memory shortages raised costs for
hardware manufacturers. Cloud and server providers, like Oracle, Dell and HPE, also highlighted strong AI-related
demand. Semis and equipment were the weakest performers. Elsewhere, entertainment and telecoms posted modest
declines. Finally, in Q3 investor attention increasingly shifted to AI agents, with Meta’s Muse, Microsoft’s Copilot and

Salesforce’s Agentforce among the prominent offerings.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Bloomberg Finance LP. Median returns taken. Updated Sep 30.

2. SEMICONDUCTORS
Chip stocks were hurt initially by a reversal of the AI trade and then by rising bond yields. The Philly SOX index fell
sharply in Q3, after its worst monthly performance in July (-21%) since 2008. Concerns about higher oil prices and
rising rates continued to weigh on the sector in Aug and Sep. SK Hynix was among the weakest performers, amid an

earnings disappointment relative to expectations and an unwinding of leveraged positions. Its Nasdaq ADRs began
trading in July. Despite these headwinds, the semis outlook remains supported by AI infrastructure demand. Within
semis, potential long-term beneficiaries include suppliers of AI infrastructure, memory including high-bandwidth
memory (HBM), advanced packaging, photonics and semiconductor equipment.

On July 30, Microsoft rose more than 15%, its strongest daily gain since 2008, following results released the previous

evening. The move added ~$450bn to its market cap, the largest one-day increase on record at the time. For context,
that daily increase is larger than the value of any company in Europe (except ASML).

3.2 MAGNIFICENT 7: Market concentration
According to our equity strategists, from end-July to mid-September, tech-exposed stocks within the S&P 500 rose
14% while the remainder declined 3%. This divergence highlights the concentration of market returns in the tech -

exposed sector. In late July, our strategists anticipated a rotation back into technology, which remains underway.

4. REGIONAL BREAKDOWN
After outperforming in Q2, mainland Chinese tech stocks posted large declines this quarter, hurt by July’s sell-off.
The CSI 300 tech series led the declines. Weakness also extended across Asia more broadly, with the KOSPI, Nikkei 225
and Hang Seng Tech down in Q3, driven by a chip sell-off and concerns over further Fed hikes. European tech equities
were more resilient, with the STOXX Europe 600 Tech Index down slightly lower in Q3 but still well above its year-end

level. Within European tech, Infineon underperformed, while Orange illustrated weakness in telecoms outside the index.

Charts of the week: No capitulation

Editorial 
Staying defensive and diversified  
Strategy summary:  Two weeks ago, we turned defensive 
towards equities, locking in semiconductor equity gains amid 
elevated bond volatility, narrowing market breadth and political 
uncertainty before the US mid -term elections. The near -term 
outlook reflected a less favourable risk-reward balance. Since 
we turned defensive , the Nasdaq 100 index has recorded its 
biggest weekly decline in three months as September’s hotter-
than-expected US inflation report briefly lifted bond yields. 
We have extended our defensive stance this week, 
downgrading US Communication Services equities to Neutral 
and upgrading US Healthcare to Overweight. We also closed 
our opportunistic bullish Communication Services idea and 
initiated a bullish idea on Healthcare, where earnings visibility 
and defensive characteristics appear more attractive. In fixed 
income, we see opportunities in US 10-year government bonds. 
We await clearer AI profit margins and spending guidance, 
broader earnings growth or lower bond and equity market 
volatility before turning positive on equities. Until then, 
diversification and disciplined entry levels remain important. 
Hot US inflation triggers equity pullback; not hot enough 
for an October Fed hike:  Risk assets pulled back after 
September’s hotter-than-expected inflation data (headline 3.7% 
y/y; core 2.6% y/y). Higher gasoline and diesel prices were the 
main drivers, but passthrough appeared in airline fares, used 
cars and transportation services, showing pressures were not 
confined to energy. Almost half of inflation components are still 
rising above 3%, a measure recently cited by Fed Chair Warsh. 
Producer inflation also beat estimates. The reports revive the 
possibility of an October Fed rate hike, but we expect a divided 
Fed to weigh it against September’s soft jobs report and 
narrowly hold this month. A December hike appears more likely 
if inflation remains firm . Although Fed Chair Warsh did not 
comment on policy at an IMF speech this week, New York Fed 
chief Williams said he saw no need to hike in 2027. Our base 
case remains two further hikes by June 2027. 
Limited equity pullback so far.  Equities fell sharply after the 
US inflation report, but the pullback is limited. The Nasdaq 100 
is 4.5% below its record high, while the S&P 500 remains close 
to all -time highs despite the latest decline. The S&P 500 
volatility index (VIX) has risen from below 15 to 1 8.2, still well 
under the 25 –30 range typically associated with capitulation 
and forced selling. Meanwhile, US government bond volatility 
(MOVE) remains elevated after surging since September.  Our 
stock-bond quant model turned more negativ e (-2) this week, 
signalling elevated equity volatility in the near term.  
When to turn more constructive on stocks?  We would turn 
more constructive when capitulation signals align: the Nasdaq 
100 approaches a 10% drawdown, the VIX rises to 25 –30 and 
our ‘Fear and Greed’ index  enters “extreme fear”. Greater 
confidence would also require easing concerns about AI 
spending and technology firms’ exposure to neocloud revenue, 
lower bond volatility and our stock-bond model turning positive 
again. Until these conditions emerge, volatility may remain 
elevated, favouring a defensive equity stance. Political risk 
should ease after the US mid -term elections. We continue to 
seek selective opportunities following excessive corrections. 
US financials remain a preferred sector after Q3 earnings:  
US large banks’ earnings were mixed, but underlying trends 
remain encouraging. Healthy loan and deposit growth supports 
net interest income, while resilient consumer spending, wealth 
management activity and a solid M&A and refinancing pipeline 
strengthen the outlook. Credit risks appear manageable . We 
remain constructive and favour stronger, diversified franchises. 
US government bonds offer value as yields spike:  The 
reversal in US government bond yields after their initial inflation-
driven spike suggests limited further upside. The 10 -year yield 
briefly reached 5.32% before slumping as falling equities 
prompted short-covering. We expect a near -term 5.00–5.25% 
range and view moves above 5.25% as attractive entry points 
for our opportunistic 10-year government bond idea. Structural 
pressures, including term premium and increasing supply to 
fund fiscal deficits and AI investments, prevent us from turning 
outright bullish on longer maturities. We, therefore, continue to 
prefer 3–7-year maturities in broader bond allocations.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions 
  How does the September 2026 US Consumer Price Index 
(CPI) report alter your view on the USD and bond yields?   
Our view: The hotter-than-expected CPI print raises the odds of 
an October Fed hike, though we still expect a hold followed by 
a rate hike in December. In the near term, we maintain our view 
that the US 10 -year government bond yield will range between 
5.00% and 5.25%, while  the USD will remain supported as 
markets reassess the likelihood of further Fed hikes. Additional 
gains would depend partly on markets pricing a faster or more 
prolonged tightening cycle, while clearer signs of an 
approaching policy rate peak could see the USD ease back. 
Rationale: September US consumer inflation  came in above 
consensus across the board – headline CPI rose 3.7% y/y (vs. 3.6% 
estimate), and core CPI rose 2.6% y/y (vs. 2.5% estimate) – marking 
the fastest headline acceleration in five months. This shifts the 
balance of risks modestly hawkish . Unsurprisingly, the market -
implied probability of an October Fed hike rose over 50% overnight 
but closed the day at 32.7% – 9ppt higher than Tuesday’s close. 
We believe a split Fed will weigh this inflation report against the soft 
September jobs report and narrowly vote to keep rates on hold this 
month. Our base case remains for two more Fed hikes  by June 
2027. A December rate hike  now looks more like a done deal, 
especially as persistently higher oil prices keep headline and core 
inflation elevated for at least the next few months. Any re-escalation 
of the Middle East conflict after the  US mid-term elections would 
exert pressure on the Fed, although we believe Republicans would  
show greater restraint in US foreign action if they suffer steep Senate 
losses, which looks increasingly likely. 
The intra-day price action in US government bonds was instructive. 
The US 10-year yield briefly spiked to 5.32% on the print before 
closing materially lower, as pre -existing bearish positioning was 
unwound and Fed speakers (Barkin, Goolsbee) stopped well short 
of signalling an imminent move. This dynamic reinforces our view 
that the technical selling pressure driving yields towards the upper 
end of our range is fading. Structural drivers – term premium, fiscal 
supply concerns and ‘higher for longer’ Fed repricing – remain intact 
and keep us from turning outright bullish on duration. However, our 
opportunistic bullish idea in US 10-year government bonds initiated 
at September-end remains, as we continue to view spikes in US 10-
year yields over 5.25% as a good risk-reward entry. From a longer-
term portfolio standpoint, we continue to favour the 3-7-year tenor. 
On FX, elevated US government bond yields should sustain the 
USD’s interest rate appeal. However, expected hikes alone will not 
drive further appreciation. Stronger data or firmer Fed guidance 
would provide fresh support, while evidence that tightening is 
nearing completion would temper the USD’s strength. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are your key takeaways from US bank earnings? 
Our view: Q3 2026 earnings results from major US banks have 
been mixed, but underlying trends remain encouraging. Healthy 
loan and deposit growth, resilient consumer spending and 
wealth management activity continue to support the sector. 
Credit risks remain manageable. We remain constructive on the 
US financial sector, favouring stronger, diversified franchises. 
Rationale: Healthy loan and deposit growth continue to support net 
interest income (NII), hinting that the US economy remains resilient. 
Capital markets revenue grew year -on-year (y/y) , but moderated 
sequentially after a very strong Q2 2026, although performance 
varied across banks. Management commentary points to a healthy 
deal pipeline , supported by M&A activity and debt refinancing 
demand, although execution will depend on market conditions. 
Provisioning and net charge -off trends were mixed across banks, 
with no clear signs of broad deterioration in credit quality. Firmer 
US inflation raises the risk of further monetary tightening. While 
higher rates can support asset yields, they can also increase 
funding costs, pressure borrowers and weigh on securities ’ 
valuations. We will continue to monitor lending demand, credit 
costs, NII trends, capital markets momentum and capital returns.  
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Can Big Tech’s earnings guidance sustain confidence in 
the AI investment cycle? 
Our view: We expect Big Tech’s AI spending to remain elevated, 
supporting our constructive AI capex outlook. However, we 
await guidance on the pace of capex growth and the 
sustainability of profit margins. 
Rationale: Semiconductor leaders’ latest company guidance points 
to supply constraints extending through 2028, supporting continued 
capacity expansion for compute . However, while AI capex is still 
likely to rise, y/y growth is expected to moderate  from c.100% in 
Q3 2026 to c.20% in Q3 2027. This normalisation in growth could 
raise concerns about the AI investment cycle’s durability.  
The Nasdaq-100 Index has rallied alongside accelerating AI capex. 
Slower capex growth is likely to moderate semiconductor revenue 
growth, while hyperscalers continue to incur depreciation costs on 
past investments. The slower growth is already partially reflected in 
the Nasdaq -100’s valuation de -rating this year. While we remain 
constructive on the AI investment theme, we prefer to stay 
prudent in the near term and await Big Tech’s guidance for greater 
visibility into spending plans in H2 2027 and beyond. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would France face a credit rating downgrade by rating 
agencies this month? What might be the market implications? 
Our view: A rating downgrade is unlikely unless France’s 2027 
budget passes the National Assembly very soon with material 
changes to the proposal. We see French government bond 
yields remaining elevated relative to German Bund yields. 
Rationale: The French National Assembly began debating France’s 
2027 draft budget on 13 October 2026, with the first vote scheduled 
for 20 October. Near-term budget approval appears unlikely, as 
opposition parties have tabled motions to reject the proposal. The 
French Parliament has up to 70 days from the budget’s submission 
to complete the legislative process, although  its final adoption and 
the resolution of political challenges could extend into early 2027.  
Last October, two of the three major rating agencies downgraded 
France by one notch to the high-single-A category  with stable 
outlooks. The remaining agenc y rated France in the low-AA 
category with a negative outlook. As negative outlooks are typically 
resolved within 12 -18 months, agenc ies have time to await fiscal 
clarity before acting. We believe downgrade risks are largely priced 
in after the recent rise in yields, but volatility is likely to persist amid 
fiscal uncertainty and the approaching presidential election. Banks 
and domestically-focused issuers would face the greatest near-term 
pressure. However, we would view wider spreads as an opportunity 
to add exposure, particularly to French banks’ subordinated 
debt, given their stronger balance sheets and the fact that France 
contributes less than 50% of major French banks’ overall revenue. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Japanese banks? 
Our view: We are positive on Japanese banks despite the recent 
market correction, as the structural drivers of improving 
profitability and shareholder returns remain intact . While 
political risk and shifting BoJ rate hike odds could sustain near-
term volatility, we see the recent pullback as an opportunity to 
build selective exposure to Japanese bank stocks. 
Rationale: Japan’s gradual monetary policy normalisation  
should support banks’ NII as floating-rate loans reprice and maturing 
assets are reinvested at higher yields. Healthy corporate lending 
demand, improving capital efficiency and continued share buybacks 
provide further support . While uncertainty over the pace of BoJ 
tightening could weigh on sentiment, we see gradual interest rate 
normalisation as supportive of medium-term bank profitability.  
Key risks  include rising deposit costs, mark -to-market losses on 
bond portfolios and a disorderly increase in Japanese government 
bond yields. We continue to favour well-capitalised banks  with 
strong domestic deposit franchises and improving returns on equity. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Could NZD/USD rebound from its one-year trough? If so, 
how far could it recover?  
Our view: New Zealand’s improving domestic conditions, 
persistent inflation and technical support provide a basis for 
NZD/USD’s initial recovery towards the 0.57 level, although 
elevated US bond yields could slow its progress. 
Rationale: In Q3, New Zealand’s  seasonally-adjusted business 
confidence reading rose to a net 40% of firms expecting the economy 
to improve, up from net 14% in Q2 . Hiring intentions improved, and 
filled jobs rose 0.2% m/m in August. These developments suggest 
domestic conditions remain supportive for the NZD.  
New Zealand’s above-target inflation keeps further RBNZ tightening 
in focus. The 22 October consumer inflation data release and the 
28 October RBNZ policy meeting are key catalysts: persistent price 
pressures a nd improving demand are expected to prompt firmer 
policy guidance and strengthen the NZD’s interest-rate appeal. 
NZD/USD’s position near the lower boundary of its downtrend 
channel suggests scope for a technical rebound. We see 0.57 as 
the first recovery hurdle; a sustained break  above this level  would 
strengthen the case for further gains. Firmer US  consumer inflation 
remains a headwind, making a gradual recovery more plausible 
than a sharp reversal.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  Can the proposed Russia -Ukraine ‘energy ceasefire ’ 
contain oil prices as Middle East risks rise? 
Our view: We expect escalating Middle East tensions to keep oil 
prices elevated . We  maintain our  three- and 12 -month West 
Texas Intermediate (WTI) oil price forecasts at USD 100/bbl and 
USD 80/bbl, respectively, and continue to favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a portfolio hedge 
against persistent inflation risks. 
Rationale: The proposed Russia-Ukraine ‘energy ceasefire’ still 
looks some way off. In our view, even if an agreement is reached, it 
would do little to ease the supply risks building in the Middle East. 
Gulf oil exports have recovered to above 80% of pre-conflict levels 
in recent weeks, helping moderate the pace of price increases. 
However, the recovery remains vulnerable to disruptions, particularly 
following the latest Houthi attacks on Saudi energy infrastructure . 
Diesel and jet fuel remain under the most pressure , with Gulf 
exports of these fuels in September  at only around 60% of pre -
conflict levels. A sustained disruption to major Saudi production or 
export infrastructure remains the principal upside risk to oil prices. 
—  Anthony Naab, Investment Strategist

技嘉 2376 TT  ( 電子–電腦及週邊設備 ) 
AI 爆發帶動 26~27 年獲利創高 
投資與評價 
1. 3Q26 在客戶資料中心完成積極拉貨下，估營收創新高，技鋼占比逾八
成，產品結構優化使毛利率明顯跳升，獲利同創新高，EPS 11.9 元。 
2. 隨 Nebius 拉貨強勁，技嘉能見度已看到 1H27，今年產能將翻倍成
長，估營收、獲利將逐季創高，2H26 獲利 YoY+172%，EPS 23 元。 
3. 預期技鋼 27 年佔比拉升至 85%，貢獻技嘉今、明年獲利再創新高，EPS
分別達 42.2 元、51.7 元，維持強力買進評等，上修目標價至 535 元。
(以稀釋後 26 年獲利估 13.5x 評價)  
重點更新 
3Q26 營收、獲利將再創高 
估技嘉 3Q26 營收 1,587 億元，QoQ+10%，YoY+100%，主要因客戶資料
中心完成積極拉貨硬體，且本季高階顯卡銷售強勁。估技鋼占比逾八成，其
中 GB 比重約 20~25%，在產品組合優化且部分低價庫存貢獻，加上消費產
品銷售回穩，估毛利率將回升至 11.4%，營業利益 YoY+245%至 118.6 億
元，上修稅後獲利 20%至 EPS 11.9 元，獲利將再創歷史新高。 
技鋼在手訂單能見度已看到 1H27 
AI 在手訂單已達 1H27，較過往一季明顯提升，主因 Nebius 硬體集中於
2H26 進駐，客戶大型合約須確保供應鏈產能，加上主權 AI 貢獻，預期
2H26 技嘉營收獲利皆翻倍成長，且營收增幅延續到 1H27，上修 4Q26 營
收至 1,742 億元，YoY+95%，Vera Rubin 將有完整貢獻，機櫃比重估三成
以上。此外，高端顯卡通路售價遠高於 MSRP，預期消費產品營收季增，毛
利率小幅回落下，EPS 12.5 元，獲利再創新高。2H26 上修獲利 21%至
163.7 億元，YoY+172%，EPS 23 元(已考量 ECB 對股本膨脹 5.97%影響) 
技嘉隨客戶 Nebius 2H26~1H27 DC 建置完成而營運增速 
Nebius 10 月全面調漲租賃價格，其中，Nvidia Blackwell 上調 19~21%，
Hooper 上調 17~20%，隨 1H27 客戶數個新資料中心落成，且繼續交付
Hyperscaler 訂單，最大供應商技嘉今年產能翻倍成長，受惠訂單強勁且確
認度提升，上修技嘉 2026 年、2027 年營收至 YoY+73%、37%，技鋼分別
年增 120%、46%，27 年佔比拉升至 85%，更多垂直整合及 GADU 加入下，
獲利持續創高，上修今、明年獲利 12%、16%至 EPS 42.16 元、51.73 元。

### Page 2

圖一:Nebius 自 10/1 調漲價格 19~21%

資料來源:Nebius，國泰證期研究部。

公司簡介 
技嘉科技(2376 TT)創立於 1986 年，以自有品牌 Gigabyte 行銷全球，主要產品包括主機板及顯
示卡，為全球前三大的板卡廠，同步發展電競筆電業務，技嘉擁有電競品牌 AORUS，另有創作
者系列筆電 Aero，持續提升高階 NB 市占。旗下技鋼主營伺服器業務，AI 佔比達九成，目前為
公司最主要成長來源。技嘉營收比重為消費產品22%，商用產品 78%。地區占比為 ASIA 58%、
America 17%、Europe 25%。

### Page 3

ESG 政策 
國泰給予技嘉 ESG 評等維持 AA，因其在環境系統與治理等構面之資訊揭露更為完備，且
資料中心產品導向以高效能、低能耗之產品為主推。優異之處在於環境系統與治理、ESG
創新、人權、多元組成與包容性。總體來說，公司在 CSR 揭露較電子同業完善，惟建議產
品製程計畫及應變流程中，可進一步揭露員工產品品質及教育訓練投入時間及成本，以利
有效掌握該構面表現及追蹤後續成效。

⚫ 供應鏈社會面向控管 (SASB 重大性議題，電子業排行 6%) 
技嘉制定 《技嘉科技永續採購準則》 ，企業社會責任面向中涵蓋勞動人權、衝突礦產、企業
道德等子目標，並訂有四項零容忍規範，包括禁童工、禁強迫勞工、禁排放未處理有害物
質及立刻造成員工身體傷害之行為。供應商稽核方面，技嘉將既有及新進供應商進行分級
管理，評比內容除了供貨合約與物料評比外，亦納入永續供應商評鑑制度，每季進行供應
商 CSR 高風險評核，初步淘汰高風險 D 級供應商，確保供應鏈的品質穩定性。此外，
2022 年技嘉亦遵循負責任商業聯盟準則(RBA)， 評鑑供應商在企業社會責任面向之表現 ，
整體平均分數達 93.5 分。衝突礦產方面，技嘉導入責任礦產計畫，調查供應商衝突礦產
使用情況，要求供應商制定明確衝突礦產管理政策，22 年技嘉採購金額前百大供應商衝
突礦產管理執行率為 73%，並持續提供輔導，以 100%為管理執行率目標。

⚫ 供應商及產品生命週期管理 (SASB 重大性議題，電子業排行 7%) 
技嘉制定 《技嘉科技永續採購準則》 ，主動採購具環保標章、能源標章、省水標章、綠建材
等全球相關綠色標章認證產品，或其製造、使用過程、廢棄處理對環境資源耗用較少、低
碳、污染程度較低之產品外，亦將符合 ISO 14001 第三方認證之環境管理體系或自行建
構獨立運作環境管理體系之供應商納入優先採購名單，並要求供應商進行環保及品質管制
並提供相關 RoHS 檢驗和測試報告，確保符合 HSF 管制要求。此外，技嘉亦依循「供應
商品質管理作業規範」根據合作狀況按月及按季評比，融入永續供應商評鑑制度，針對表
現不好者提供輔導或汰換處理，遴選出優良廠商頒發永續廠商獎，促進廠商在永續績效上
形成良性競爭共 82.21%的供應商參與永續性整合評鑑。供應商輔導方面，技嘉制定減量
333 計畫，鼓勵供應商以每年減水、碳、廢棄物各 3%為目標，藉由供應商大會作為廠商
間交流永續作為的平台外，提供實務訓練進行輔導，22 年共 130 家供應商響應計畫，其
中 55 家提供量化績效。產品生命週期管理方面，技嘉導入碳足跡及水足跡盤查，評估產
品對環境衝擊。

### Page 4

原物料與廢棄物管理 (SASB 重大性議題，電子業排行 7%)  
技嘉於永續報告書揭露當年度原物料使用量，並透過精簡包裝、提高可回收材料比例及採
用保麗龍回收料等方式，降低包材物料使用量。技嘉自 2011 年開始分析包材回收率與成
分，透過筆電研發設計部門進行筆電包材材積優化改良，經推估不同系列產品材積減少  
19-52% 不等，產品包材的總重量較 2011 年減少 60.43%，持續以 2030 年起不使用一
次性包裝材做為最終目標。

廢棄物管理方面，技嘉制定廢棄物管理政策，並揭露一般及有害事業廢棄物和回收總量。
技嘉亦制定具體減量目標，以持續追蹤廢棄物管理績效，目標每年減量達3%，並於 2030
年達成較 2010 年減量 50%目標。針對電子廢棄物，技嘉透過子公司參照荷蘭環境評估署
定義的 9R 循環經濟主張，進行評估分析後進行維修及整新，2022 年共為 660,710 件商
品延續生命，相當於減少 774.2 公噸的電子廢棄物。

有害物質管理方面，技嘉建置有害物質管制規範，區分禁用物質、限期禁用物質及未來潛
在禁用物質三級有害物質，系統性管理有害物質清單，並針對危險性即時應對。此外，公
司亦於每季綠色永續發展委員會中呈報國際法規變動情形，使技嘉可即時針對有害物質使
用的國際趨勢進行調整。

ESG 創新 (SASB 重大性議題，電子業排行 9%)  
技嘉設置綠色永續發展委員會，由董事長擔任主席，1~2 個月召開會議討論永續、環境或
產品相關議題。綠色認證方面，技嘉成立子公司百事益，建立永續循環經濟商業模式，百
事益於 22 年取得 BS 8001 循環經濟標準、商業模式成熟度最佳化認證，榮獲第三屆台灣
循環經濟獎的創新服務獎傑出獎及 SGS 品質卓越案例獎。綠色設計方面，技嘉針對產品
進行高效率低耗能的設計與改良，透過於產品生命週期中導入各式創新材料、技術和管理
機制，促成產品高效能與耐用的表現。技嘉亦因應資料中心需求贈加，自 21 年開始協助
客戶建置不同浸沒式冷卻運算機房，有效降低 90%耗能。

智慧生產方面，技嘉亦提出倉儲物流自動化解決方案，導入無人車使生產過程更加效率及
安全，且在疫情期間成功減緩人力短缺問題。智慧財產管理方面，技嘉制訂《技嘉集團智
財權綱領》 及 《技嘉集團專利管理辦法》 ，以擬定智慧財產策略，並保護關鍵技術。此外，
亦透過「技嘉科技集團專利獎勵辦法」 、辦理教育訓練與提案競賽，激發內部員工創新意
識，累積至 22 年已取得 2832 項專利，並有 104 人獲頒內部專利獎勵。

⚫ ESG 創新 (SASB 重大性議題 電子業排行 9%) 
技嘉成立隸屬於總裁室之綠色永續發展委員會，作為永續發展事務推動的統籌決策和運作
組織委員會職責為氣候治理策略、價值鏈合作及具體減緩與調適計畫，每個月至少召開一
次會議，由各組織代表會報永續、環境或產品法規與趨勢，提出公司應對策略以求即時因
應國際脈動。製程創新方面，技嘉執行 19 項製程智能化，包含產線配置與流程優化、設
備改善開發、作業輔具導入等 ，將智能系統技術運用於生產過程中。為促進循環經濟，技
嘉 2018 年成立百事益國際 ， 透過 「以租代買」 商業模式 、 整新品再售 、 開拓過季品市場 ，
推動「全球甜檸檬認證標章」的逆物流整合服務，以期解決買賣雙方資訊不對稱導致低價
劣品充斥的「酸檸檬市場」 。法律遵循方面，近 3 年未意圖扭曲市場正常運作而受公平交
易委員會懲罰，未與供應商訴訟情事產生。

### Page 5

ESG 可加強處 
產品安全 (電子業排行 83%)  
產品安全方面，技嘉推行全面品質管理，以達成環境保護與客戶滿意，品質政策包括 ISO 
9001／IECQ QC 080000， 其中 ， 技嘉符合IATF 16949：2016 汽車業品質管理系統和 ISO 
9001 的品質管理等相關認證。惟建議技嘉可以透過自行設立實驗室或委託第三方公正實
驗室抽樣產品品質，以確保產品品質。此外，亦建議公司揭露員工產品品質方面之教育訓
練具體投入時間或費用，以利利害關係人瞭解公司於提升產品品質之投入。

風險及危機管理 (SASB 重大性議題，電子業排行 72%)  
風險及危機管理方面，技嘉依循風險控管流程鑑別出十大面向風險，並評估衝擊程度及發
生機率後繪製成風險矩陣，針對各類風險訂定管控政策，惟建議技嘉可以制定具體稅務治
理政策，以降低各項營運產生之稅賦成本。

資訊安全方面，技嘉設置資訊安全委員會，由總經理擔任最高管理層級，在資訊管理政策
上遵循國際資安標準(NIST CSF 框架)及國內外法規。惟建議技嘉導入 ISO 27001 相關資
訊安全管理系統，並取得第三方認證，且建議公司針對資訊或網路安全至少每年一次定期
進行檢視或測試，及說明最近一次評估結果，。此外，建議技嘉可以揭露員工資訊安全相
關教育訓練之訓練時數或完訓率，以利利害關係人瞭解公司於維護資訊安全之投入。

地產 中立 升息對REITs喜憂參半，轉注意現金流成長動能。
1
《升息不利地產REITs，但租賃期短者表現較佳》
利率變動會直接牽動房地產投資信託基金 (REITs)的
融資成本增加 ，壓縮可分配現金流與股利 ，依高盛

研究報告顯示，從2006年起的10年期美國國債收益
率上升至少40個基點的滾動六個月週期內，REITs的
表現比標普 500指數低 6.7%，過去五年 ，大多數
REIT 與 10 年期公債殖利率呈負相關，主要因為受
到租賃期間長短的影響 ，其中以飯店業及倉儲設施
因租賃期間較短可當下反應租金變動 ，購物中心因
經濟表現佳 ，可支配所得增加 ，所以表現反而較佳 ；
淨租賃、醫療保健REITs表現最差，但以近期而言，
業績 及營運模式的調整重要性也上升 ，醫療 保健
REITs受個別基本面不同，部分業者營運模式從過去
較長天期的淨租賃轉往養老住宅 ，縮短租賃期，老
年人口財富負擔能力提升，支撐老人住房市場定價
資料來源：高盛，2026/9/18，中國信託商業銀行彙整，
2026/10/1

力，租金增加 ，入住率也提升接近 90%；資料中心

《預期地產交易價格降溫 ，4Q26交易量增長放緩 》

Green Street CPPI 數據顯示，全美商業地產價格
相較於低點已呈現微幅回升 ，8月上漲0.8%。過去
一年累計上漲5%，但美國國債收益率的上升及對借
貸成本的影響 ，恐促使購房者重新考慮支付意願 ，
預計漲勢即將降溫。依高盛統計截至8月的季度累計
地產交易量年增 18%(扣除合併 )，高於世邦魏理仕
和仲量聯行對 3Q26投資銷售額年增 13%和14%的
預期，8月份的強勁成長主要由工業地產和辦公地產
推動，而多戶住宅市場仍然落後 。工業倉儲主要受
惠供應過剩逐漸下降 ，租賃活動受到美國製造業回
流、近岸外包與第三方物流供應鏈重組支撐 ，優質
近港與門戶型物流中心空置率開始回落；辦公室物
業則呈現科技與專業服務企業偏好承租一線 A級商
辦，而不青睞 B/C級商辦 ，拆除舊型辦公室 、改建
住宅等 。多戶住宅仍在消化陽光帶 (德州、佛州等)
過剩的存量，壓抑短期租金漲幅 。受30年期固定房
貸利率在9月升至7%的高位及去年基期也高的影響 ，
預期4Q26交易量放緩。

《REITs股息吸引力低，轉注意現金流成長動能》
美REITs的回報在利率下降時會擴張估值，但現美國
10年期公債殖利率多 在5%以上，美國REITs平均股
息收益率約3.9%，相對不具投資吸引力 ，對房地產
持中立。然若考量租金現金流增長 ，租金定價力相
對強勢的族群主要為養老住宅 ，同店淨營利年增率
可達20%上下，但估值偏高，建議回檔可留意。

Document 452: 今年度も上振れが予想される税収 〜政府見通し通りの名目経済成長率達成で90兆円台到達の可能性～

Source PDF: all report pdf/今年度も上振れが予想される税収 〜政府見通し通りの名目経済成長率達成で90兆円台到達の可能性～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年9 月 28 日(月)

1/5 
今年度も上振れが予想される税収 
〜政府見通し通りの名目経済成長率達成で 90 兆円台到達の可能性～ 
 経済調査部 首席エコノミスト  永濱 利廣 
（℡：050-5474-7492）

（要旨） 
○ 税収は当初予算対比で5 年連続上振れしており、2025 年度決算は84.2 兆円に到達。物価高や株
高、高い欠損法人割合（60.3％）の低下余地を背景に、実質的な税収弾性値（過去平均約2.2）
は政策当局の想定より高く推移している。内閣府の経済成長率見通し（+3.0％）を基に試算す
ると、2026 年度税収は89.8 兆円に達し、内閣府見通しを＋6.1 兆円上回る可能性がある。 
○ 2026 年 4-6 月期の一般政府債務残高/GDP 比は200％を割り込み、純債務比率は四半世紀前の水
準まで改善。2024・2025 年度の低下幅（各▲14.6%pt）の5 割以上はインフレ率要因であり、物
価上昇の定着が財政の持続可能性を高めている。 
○ 過度な緊縮は経済成長を損なうため、インフレに伴う改善分を活用した適切な財政運営が必
要。具体的には、食料品の消費税減税、設備投資の即時償却、所得連動型給付などの支出を伴
う再分配策を実施し、潜在成長率の引き上げと賃上げの継続を図るべきである。 
●はじめに 
税収の上振れが続いている。 実際、 当初予算との対比でみれば、2021～2025 年度にかけて５年連
続で上振れており、平均上振れ額は＋5.9 兆円にも上る（図表１）。

### Page 2

2/5 
具体的に直近 25 年度の税収を見ると、 当初予算時点で77.8 兆円だったのが、 補正後予算で80.7
兆円に上方修正されている。そして決算時点に至っては84.2 兆円にまで上振れしている。 
特に注目すべきは、2025 年度の名目ＧＤＰ成長率は＋4.3％となったが、24 年度の大規模定額減
税の反動もあり税収は＋12.0％も増加したことである。 つまり、 名目ＧＤＰが１％変化したときに
税収が何％変化するかを示す税収弾性値は、定額減税の反動増も含まれるものの 25 年度は 2.8 と
なり、これまで一般的に 1.1～1.2 とされてきたことからすると、少なくとも近年は政策当局の想
定よりも高いことになる（図表２）。

この背景としては、 物価や株価が上昇していることや、 所得税が累進課税になっていることに加
え、繰越欠損金や欠損法人割合の変化等が指摘できる。実際に、過去の欠損法人割合は名目ＧＤＰ
成長率と非常に連動性が高くなっている（図表３） 。

### Page 3

3/5 
そして、過去には 50％を切る時期もあった欠損法人割合が、直近2024 年度時点でも依然として
60.3％の水準にあることからすれば、 当面は欠損法人割合の低下が期待されることによって、税収
弾性値が高水準を維持することが予想される。

●今年度の税収は見通し通りの経済成長で＋6.1 兆円程度上振れの可能性 
そこで以下では、デフレに突入して欠損法人割合の水準が高まった 98 年度以降の平均的な税収
弾性値を計算してみた。 すると、 過去の関係に基づけば、98～2024 年度の平均的な税収弾性値は約
2.2 となる（図表４）。つまり、欠損法人割合がまだ低下する余地がある局面では、名目ＧＤＰが
１％増加すると、 政府が想定するよりも1.8（＝2.2/1.2） 倍程度の税収の伸びが平均的に期待でき
ることを示している。

（出所）内閣府、財務省データを元に筆者作成

一方、 ８月に内閣府が改定した「中長期の経済財政に関する試算（以下、 内閣府試算） 」 に基づけ
ば、26 年度の名目GDP 成長率は＋3.0％となっている。 つまり、 仮に26 年度の名目成長率が政府の
見通し通りとなり、税収弾性値を 98 年度以降の平均となる 2.2 程度になるとすれば、今年度の税
収は、昨年度の84.2 兆円から＋3.0％×2.2＝＋6.6％程度増加する計算となる。 
つまり、この結果を用いて今年度の税収を計算すると、84.2 兆円×1.066＝89.8 兆円となる。そ
して、これを先の内閣府試算における税収見通しと比較すれば、26 年度の 83.7 兆円を＋6.1 兆円
程度上回ることになる。 
従って、 今後の税収を見通すうえでは、 少なくとも欠損法人割合にまだ低下余地がある局面では
過少推計になる可能性があり、これに伴う税収弾性値の上振れも考慮に入れるべきと考えられる。

### Page 4

4/5 
●大幅改善する政府債務残高/GDP 比 
一方、ストック面からみても、近年の財政指標は改善が続いている。日銀の資金循環統計によれ
ば、26 年4－6 月期の一般政府債務残高／ＧＤＰ比は200％を割り込んでいる（図表５）。また、純
債務／ＧＤＰ比に至っては、実に四半世紀前の水準まで下がっている。背景には、円安に伴う法人
税収増や物価高に伴う消費税収増、 さらにはブラケットクリープ現象や株高金利上昇等に伴う所得
税収増などがある。そこで続いては、インフレが財政に及ぼす影響について検討し、今後の政策対
応について考えてみたい。

●債務残高/GDP 比低下の主因はインフレ 
そもそも財政の健全性を判断する国際標準的な指標は先に見た政府債務残高／GDP 比であり、こ
れまで日本の財政目標となってきた国・地方のプライマリーバランス（以下 PB）は、2025 年度時
点で GDP 比▲0.0%とほぼ均衡している。 
そして、政府債務残高をＤ、名目GDP をＹ、基礎的財政収支をＰＢ、長期金利をｒ、名目経済成
長率をｇとすると、政府債務残高／GDP と PB の関係は以下の式で与えられることが知られている。

Ｄ／Ｙ＝ＰＢ／Ｙ＋（１＋ｒ）Ｄ(-1)／（１＋ｇ）Ｙ(-1)

そして、 以上の式を用いて、 政府債務残高/GDP 比が低下した要因について、 その前年差を基礎的
財政収支要因と利払い費要因を合わせた「インフレ率要因」 「経済成長率要因」 「財政収支等要因」
に分解した結果をみると、2024・25 年度の低下幅 （いずれも▲14.6％ポイント）のうち、５割以上
がインフレ率要因であることがわかる（図表６）。なお、24・25 年度は単年の改善幅としても、現
基準の資金循環統計が開始された 98 年以降で最大となっている。この背景には、日本でもインフ

### Page 5

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
5/5 
レが定着したことがあり、財政の持続可能性にもその分余裕が出てきているといえる。

●インフレ局面で求められる財政運営 
今後の青写真としては、 景気回復局面ではインフレに伴う財政改善分の一部を適切に活用しなが
ら、政府債務残高／ＧＤＰ比の低下を維持した財政運営がベストだろう。しかし、景気が後退局面
に陥り、 金融緩和余地も限られた状況になれば、 財政運営も政府債務残高／ＧＤＰの低下よりもマ
クロ経済の安定化が重視される機能的財政アプローチへのシフトが望ましいと言えよう。 特に近年
の世界的なインフレ等により、家計部門から政府や企業部門への所得移転が生じやすい局面では、
効果的な再分配政策が必要と考えられる。 
具体的なメニューとしては、 海外では常識となっている所得税の課税最低限引き上げやブラケッ
トシフトが望ましいが、 制度導入に時間もかかる。 効果的と考えられるのは支出に伴う再分配であ
る。 食料品の消費税減税に加え、 国内設備投資に対する即時償却減税や就業者に対する所得連動型
給付措置を行えば、物価高対策に加えて資本や労働投入量、労働生産性が高まる可能性があり、潜
在成長率の引き上げにも効果的と考えられる。 
また、長期停滞からの脱却を確実なものとするために、来年の春闘も重要である。政府による更
なる春闘への働きかけや企業の賃上げに対するインセンティブを誘発させる政策も必要になって
くるだろう。 
結局、失われた 30 年からの脱却の芽を開花までつなげることができれば、政府債務の縮小を重
視した財政運営も実施しやすくなるが、逆に失敗してしまうと失われた 30 年からの本格的な脱却
は困難になろう。 そういう意味でも、インフレ局面での財政運営は締めすぎず出しすぎないような
バランス感覚が、これまで以上に必要ではないかと考えられる。

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年10 月 13 日

1/4 
企業物価は鈍化も、上昇圧力はなお強い（26 年 9 月企業物価指数） 
～川下への価格転嫁が続く。資源高による再加速リスクも～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

（要旨） 
○ ９月の国内企業物価指数は前月比▲0.3％、前年比＋6.8％（８月：前年比＋7.6％）。 前年比
上昇率は４ヵ月ぶりに＋７％を下回ったが、農林水産物や石油製品の下落による影響が大き
く、企業段階の物価上昇圧力はなお根強い。 
○ 川上・川中では価格上昇に一服感がみられる一方、川下のプラスチック製品では高い伸びが
続いている。既往のコスト高の価格転嫁はなお進んでおり、川下への物価上昇圧力は依然と
して強い。 
○ ＡＩ関連需要の拡大を背景に、半導体や銅などの価格上昇も目立つ。資源価格とは異なる経
路からの物価上昇圧力として注目される。 
○ 先行きは、川下への価格転嫁が続くなか、資源価格の高止まりによって川上からの上昇圧力
が再び強まるリスクがある。企業物価は当面高止まりし、消費者物価についても年度後半に
かけて上昇率を明確に高める可能性が高い。

1. 前年比は＋６％台に鈍化も、物価上昇圧力はなお根強い 
日本銀行から公表された2026 年９月の国内企業物価指数は、前月比▲0.3％、前年比＋6.8％と
なった。前月比では２ヵ月連続で下落し、前年比でも４ヵ月ぶりに＋７％を下回った。８月の前
年比＋7.6％から大きく鈍化しており、春先の急激な物価上昇には一服感がみられる。 
もっとも、企業段階での物価上昇圧力が大きく和らいだわけではない。今月は農林水産物や石
油・石炭製品の下落が全体の前月比を押し下げた一方、プラスチック製品など川下への価格転嫁
は続き、ＡＩ関連の価格上昇も目立った。また、足元の資源価格の高止まりを踏まえると、先行
きは川上からの上昇圧力が再び強まるリスクもあることにも注意が必要だ。 
前月比の内訳をみると、農林水産物が同▲8.8％（寄与度：▲0.42％ポイント）と大幅に下落し
た。玄米や精米、豚肉などが主な下落要因となった。米については、2026 年産米の概算金の引き
下げなどを背景に価格が大きく低下したとみられる。農林水産物は前年比でも▲13.3％と大幅な
マイナスとなっており、９月の企業物価を押し下げる大きな要因となった。

### Page 2

2/4 
また、石油・石炭製品も前月比▲2.2％と下落した（寄与度：▲0.15％ポイント） 。重油、軽
油、ジェット燃料油などの下落が影響している。農林水産物と石油・石炭製品のマイナス寄与は
合計▲0.57％ポイントと、国内企業物価全体の前月比▲0.3％を大きく上回っている。この２つを
除けばプラス寄与となっており、企業物価の下落が一部に集中していたことが分かる。 
実際、プラスチック製品が前月比＋1.1％、金属製品が同＋0.7％、化学製品が同＋0.3％、飲食
料品が同＋0.2％、電子部品・デバイスが同＋0.4％となるなど、多くの工業製品で価格上昇がみ
られた。また、報道によれば、日銀が公表する515 品目のうち、前年比で価格が上昇した品目は
460 品目と、全体の約９割に達している。国内企業物価の前年比上昇率は鈍化したものの、値上が
りは依然として幅広い品目に及んでいる。企業段階での物価上昇圧力はなお根強いとみるべきだ
ろう。

2. 川中価格は下落も、川下への価格転嫁は続く 
石油化学関連では、春先にみられた急激な価格上昇の反動が続いている。石油・石炭製品は前
月比▲2.2％と、３ヵ月連続で下落した。ベンゼンが同＋17.2％、キシレンが同＋6.0％と、川上
の石油化学系基礎製品の一部で上昇もみられるが、ベンゼンについては中国の製油所の定期修理
に伴う供給減少という個別要因も影響しているようだ。川上価格全体でみれば、このところやや
落ち着きもみられる。 
川中に位置する合成樹脂は前月比▲0.8％と、８月の同▲2.7％に続いて下落した。内訳をみて
も、ポリエチレンが同▲2.9％、ポリプロピレンが同▲0.2％となるなど、主要品目で下落が続い
ている。４月の川上価格急騰を受けて５～６月には合成樹脂への価格波及が進んだが、夏場以降
は反落が続いている。川中段階での追加的なコスト上昇圧力は弱まりつつあると言えそうだ。 
これに対して、川下価格の動きは対照的である。プラスチック製品は９月も前月比＋1.1％と上

### Page 3

3/4 
昇し、前年比では＋12.8％と８月の＋11.3％からさらに伸びを高めた。川中の合成樹脂価格が２
ヵ月連続で下落する一方、その先に位置するプラスチック製品では前年比上昇率がむしろ加速し
ており、川中と川下の価格動向の乖離が一段と鮮明になった。 
品目別にみても、価格上昇は最終製品に近い段階で続いている。９月はプラスチック製容器
（中空成形を除く）が前月比＋11.2％と大幅に上昇するなど、容器類を中心に価格上昇が続い
た。こうした包装資材は食品や日用品など幅広い商品に用いられるため、川下企業のコスト負担
を高める要因となる。 
こうした川中と川下の価格動向の違いの背景には、企業間の価格転嫁に時間差があることが挙
げられる。原材料価格が低下しても、川下企業の仕入れコストが直ちに下がるわけではない。高
値で調達した原材料の在庫が残っているほか、取引価格の改定も一定の間隔で行われるためであ
る。加えて、物流費や人件費など原材料以外のコスト上昇も続いている。９月の「合成樹脂の下
落とプラスチック製品の上昇」という組み合わせは、既往のコスト高の価格転嫁が川下でなお続
いていることを示すものといえる。 
また、今回の企業物価では、ＡＩ関連需要の拡大を背景とする価格上昇も注目される。９月の
輸出物価指数は契約通貨ベースで前月比＋1.4％となったが、このうち電気・電子機器の寄与度は
＋0.80％ポイントと、上昇の半分以上を占めた。輸入物価でもモス型メモリ集積回路や銅鉱など
が上昇しており、世界的なＡＩ・データセンター需要の拡大による影響がうかがわれる。ＡＩ関
連需要の拡大は日本の輸出や企業収益にとって追い風となる一方、半導体や銅などの調達コスト
上昇を通じて、国内の関連製品価格を押し上げる側面もある。資源価格とは異なる経路からの物
価上昇圧力として、今後の動向が注目される。

3. 川下転嫁と資源高で、物価上昇圧力は長期化へ 
先行きについては、既往のコスト高の川下への価格転嫁が今後もしばらく続く可能性が高い。
川中の合成樹脂価格が下落する一方、川下のプラスチック製品では値上がりが続いており、原材
料価格の低下が川下価格の押し下げにつながるまでには、なお時間を要するとみられる。物流費
や人件費などを含めた企業のコスト水準も依然として高く、価格転嫁の動きは簡単には収まらな
いだろう。 
加えて、資源価格の再上昇による新たなコスト上昇圧力にも注意が必要だ。９月の輸入物価指
数は円ベースでは前月比▲0.2％と小幅な下落となったものの、契約通貨ベースでは同＋1.0％と
上昇に転じた。なかでも石油・石炭・天然ガスは契約通貨ベースで同＋2.3％、円ベースでも同＋
0.9％と上昇している。資源価格再上昇の影響がラグをもって輸入物価押し上げにつながること
で、10 月の輸入物価は上昇に転じる可能性が高まっている。こうした動きが国内の石油・化学関
連価格に時間差を伴って波及すれば、川上からの物価上昇圧力が再び強まることになる。 
このため、国内企業物価の前年比上昇率は９月に＋６％台へ鈍化したものの、今後も当面＋
６％台を中心とした高い伸びが続く可能性が高い。農林水産物の価格下落は引き続き企業物価の
押し下げ要因となる可能性がある一方、既往のコスト高の川下転嫁が続くなか、資源価格や為替

### Page 4

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
4/4
を通じた新たなコスト上昇が重なれば、企業物価の明確な鈍化には時間を要するだろう。
消費者物価への影響にも注意が必要である。米価格の下落は食料品価格を押し下げる要因とな
るものの、米以外の食料品では上昇圧力が強まる可能性が高い。プラスチック製容器など包装資
材の価格上昇が続いているほか、物流費や人件費なども高止まりしており、こうしたコスト増が
時間差を伴って最終販売価格へ転嫁されるとみられる。米価下落が一定の下押し要因となるなか
でも、食料品全体では年度後半にかけて価格上昇圧力が強まると予想している。
総じてみれば、９月の企業物価は、農林水産物や石油製品の下落によって全体の上昇率が鈍化
したものの、川下への価格転嫁はなお続き、ＡＩ関連の価格上昇も目立つ結果となった。先行き

は、既往のコスト高の価格転嫁が続くなか、資源価格の高止まりによって川上からの上昇圧力が
再び強まるリスクもある。企業物価は当面高い伸びが続く可能性が高いだろう。
消費者物価についても、既往のコスト高の価格転嫁が続くことで、上昇圧力が強まるとみられ
る。筆者は、26 年末から27 年初にかけて消費者物価上昇率が前年比＋３％を上回ると予想してお
り、今回の企業物価指数もこうした見通しと整合的な結果だったと考えている。当面、物価の上
振れに警戒が必要な局面が続くだろう。
